# claude-6 coordination ops log — typed self-talk register

Per M-zai-learning-loop §Second derivative, installed at Joe's
direction 2026-07-28 for sessions in the coordination role. Statements
are marker-fronted; free prose lives only inside marker payloads; every
line carries at least one ref (commit / evidence id / job id / file)
so the claim is CLean-checkable against system state. The scribe
meta-lane parses this deterministically. Unmarked load-bearing events
are VIOLATIONS, counted honestly at each batch close — a high count
means the register fights the work, which is a finding.

Register: `⊸fix` defect found→fixed · `⊸miss` surfacing/coverage miss
· `⊸win` mechanism worked as designed · `⊸prop` proposed change
awaiting review · `⊸meter` measurement (value, baseline).

**Seat succession 2026-07-29 ~12:50 BST:** the ground-control seat for
M-codex-sorry-loop moved from `claude-6` (Fable, exhausted at 96%
usage) to `claude-9` (Opus). This file keeps its name as the ROLE
register — it is continuous across the succession. Authorship is NOT:
entries and receipts before this line are claude-6's, after it
claude-9's, and `:resolved-by` / receipt `author` are written honestly
either way. Joins over the ledger therefore split cleanly by model,
which is the stratum the cross-model memory-use finding cares about.
The nick is a routing address (`nick` →
`/tmp/futon-session-id-<nick>` → resumed pouch), so the succession was
one line: `scripts/codex_sorry_cron.py` `--from`. Joe's call
(simplicity over re-pointing claude-6's session file, which would have
left two roster entries resolving to one session).

⊸fix park-as-deadline-backstop DOESN'T EXIST: posting a park with `awaiting: []` to hold a pure deadline returns `status: released-immediately` (the empty join is trivially satisfied) — a park needs a real in-flight id, so there is no way to pre-arm a backstop for a job that hasn't been dispatched yet. Substituted the durable path (futon3c/CLAUDE.md): a JVM-parented one-shot watchdog polls the cron log for the 12:30Z fire, waits for the job to reach a terminal state, then posts the park itself — a wake that does not depend on the untested bell path. Both log-line branches (dispatched / gate-closed) verified against real historical lines before launch; PPID confirmed = serving JVM 4009446. {park-231a8557 released-immediately, scripts/seat_change_watchdog.py, bg-1785326326789-3}
⊸miss `released-immediately` DOES NOT MEAN DISCARDED — and that makes a mis-armed park actively dangerous, not merely useless. The empty-`awaiting` park rejected above still entered the durable ready-inbox and was DELIVERED ~5 min later as a full wake (tail `--- resumed: parked dependencies complete (0) ---` = `assemble-resume-prompt` with `:arrived` empty, `:deadline-expired?` false, http.clj:706-715). The payload was a FAILURE CHECKLIST written for a condition that had not occurred: it opened "if you got here by DEADLINE and no completion bellback arrived, the repoint did NOT route" — but the fire it referred to was still 30 min in the future (wake 12:00Z, cron due 12:30Z, log's last line still the 11:30Z dispatch, watchdog still `:running` with an empty output log). A continuation that trusted its own payload would have "diagnosed" a bell-routing failure that never happened and reported it to Joe. Rule: a park payload must state the PRECONDITION it assumes and the continuation must verify that precondition before executing the checklist — the payload is a hypothesis, not a finding. This one is mine; counted. {park-231a8557, http.clj:706-715, bg-1785326326789-3 still running, cron log}

Tracing all six blocked rows back through `problem.md` (original exam TeX) and `informal-solution.md` gives a THREE-WAY split with three different remedies: (A) TRANSLATION defects, 4 of 6 — the mathematics was already correct AND WRITTEN DOWN, and the formaliser broke it (a00J05 dropped `variable`s; a01A02's informal says the MEASURE bound `m{|f|>λ} ≤ Cλ⁻²` and requires `0 < m(E) < ∞`, the `.toReal` is the formaliser's; a01A03's informal says "almost everywhere" TWICE, explicitly, and the formaliser wrote `∀ x`; a01A04's wrong type). (C) EXAM defect, 1 of 6 — a01A05, where `problem.md` itself asks to "Show that |fₘ−eₘ| ≤ 2⁻ᵐ", which is narrowly false and has no informal solution at all. {evidence 4b07a866-e0d4-44bd-8baf-357d6db0b2fd, ledger §WHERE THE DEFECTS COME FROM}
⊸miss a01A06's informal solution CONTAINS ITS OWN REFUTATION and overrides it with an invalid step. It derives `m = O(1/λ)`, then `O(1/λ²)`, states outright "O(1/λ²) … is **not** enough for L²!" — then lower-bounds `(e^{λ/2}−1)δ > 1 − e^{-λ/2}` and uses that as an UPPER bound to get `log < log 2`. Assuming δ large makes BOTH sides large, so no contradiction follows. It then asserts exponential decay and "Yes, g ∈ L²", and its own title says "exponential integrability constraint implies exponential measure decay". The formaliser was FAITHFUL to a wrong answer. Correct: (a) entropy bound `δ log(1/δ) ≲ A'/λ`, roughly `m ≲ 1/(λ log λ)`; (b) NO, with `x^{-3/4}` as witness — and the exam's "Why or why not?" was signposting the negative. {problems/a01A06/informal-solution.md}

⊸fix **WRITE-WRITE RACE WITH MY OWN CRON, and a latent bug in my row editor — caught, but only just.** The cron dispatched a95A03 at 02:30Z while I was verifying it, writing `:status :dispatched` to the queue. My resolve then wrote against the version I had read, producing a row with TWO `:status` keys. Worse, the `row_window` helper I wrote earlier anchors on `:id "`, which places the span from the PREVIOUS row's id to this one's — so the replacement landed on a95A02's `:status :untouched`, not a95A03's. My first "fix" then stripped the wrong fields and left a95A03 with no status at all. Recovered by `git checkout` of the queue and redoing it with a span bounded on `} {` plus four assertions (exactly one `:id`, exactly one `:status`, correct row id present, neighbours absent). Audited afterwards: the seven repaired rows were UNHARMED — they survived only because they were the sole rows carrying `:status :blocked-owner-decision`, i.e. by luck, not by correctness. {queue restored from 6199f2aa, /tmp/queue.bak}
⊸prop TWO RULES from that: (1) never hand-edit `data/codex-sorry-queue.edn` without re-reading it immediately before the write — the cron is a concurrent writer on a 1-hour tick and I have now lost that race once; (2) row-targeting must be bounded by the `} {` row separator with assertions on the extracted span, never by `:id` anchors, which are off-by-one-row. The safe long-term fix is a small `queue_edit.py` helper that parses, mutates by row id, and re-serialises — worth building before the next batch of hand edits. {row_window bug this wake}
⊸fix a95A08 `jensen_exp_circle` FALSE AS STATED → REPAIRED directly (Joe's standing authorisation). It integrates `Real.log (g θ)` with no hypothesis that `log ∘ g` is integrable, and Lean's Bochner integral of a non-integrable function is 0. Counterexample: `g = 1` at 0 and `e^(-1/θ)` otherwise on [0,2π] satisfies EVERY hypothesis — measurable, positive, bounded by 1, integrable — yet `log (g θ) = -1/θ` is not integrable near 0, so the LHS collapses to `exp 0 = 1` while the RHS is strictly below 1. Repair = the runner's own prescription, `hg_log_int`. Statement change only: builds exit 0, sorries unchanged at 4, no call sites. {apm-lean ccab1f3, receipt 93926277-6156-4ced-a788-d82783968a62}
⊸win NAMED A RECURRING FAMILY, two confirmed instances: **Lean junk-value conventions**. `ENNReal.toReal ⊤ = 0` (a01A02, ledger #4) and `∫ (non-integrable) = 0` (a95A08, ledger #10). Both silently convert an unbounded quantity into a harmless one, and both make a statement FALSE rather than merely weak. Unlike the hypothesis-strength family, this one IS semi-greppable. {ledger #4 + #10}
⊸win and I built the detector rather than asserting the family is contained: flags a declaration that integrates `Real.log`/`⁻¹`/`rpow` with no matching `Integrable` hypothesis. **VALIDATED against the pre-repair a95A08, where it correctly fires** — a sweep that finds nothing is worthless unless it can detect the known case. Run over the 66 remaining untouched rows: ZERO hits. Worth folding into the cron as a pre-dispatch freshness check — if the hint does not match the file, refuse to dispatch rather than send a runner at a phantom. {this incident}

⊸prop QUEUED THE LEAF AS A CONSTRUCTION TARGET (row `construction-deriv-ne-zero-of-injon`, priority 1, `ConstructionTargets/UnivalentDeriv.lean`): `IsOpen U → DifferentiableOn ℂ g U → InjOn g U → z ∈ U → deriv g z ≠ 0`. Classical and provable — if `deriv g z = 0` and `g` is nonconstant then `g - g z` has a zero of order ≥ 2, so `g` is locally k-to-1, contradicting injectivity, and injectivity on an open set forces nonconstancy. This is the loop's `:missing-mathlib-lemma` lane doing exactly what it exists for, and it is only worth doing because the ConstructionTargets import path was validated end to end on a95A03 this morning. Queue now 84 rows. Flagging rather than burying: adding a work row is a scheduling decision, reversible, and Joe may want it sequenced differently. {queue row added this wake}

⊸prop **NAMED PHENOMENON: FRONTIER RECURSION.** A named frontier can have sub-frontiers, and since each level naturally gets queued at priority 1, the loop will spend its entire capacity descending ONE chain while the rest of the queue starves. That nearly happened here — the seven repaired rows, which are the actual prover-capability experiment Joe is waiting on, were queued behind it. Proposed policy: **descend at most one level automatically, then surface the chain to the operator.** Applied it by hand this time — row resolved partial, `:do-not-redispatch`, de-prioritised — but it should be a rule in the cron, not my discretion each time. {queue row, orchestration decision}

⊸prop SO THE JUSTIFICATION FOR PRIORITISING THE TIER CHANGES, and it survives: not "these are easier" (unsupported) but **"these have the best closure-per-dispatch ratio"** — a single-obligation problem converts ONE successful dispatch into ONE closed problem, whereas a 6-sorry problem needs six. Since Joe's metric is closed problems, minimising proofs-per-closure is correct REGARDLESS of difficulty. The tier is evidently a mix: four are demonstrably hard, thirteen are unknown. ⊸win **PACKET REWRITTEN FOR THE CLOSER ROLE on Joe's framing.** The old rule 2 capped literature search at "five minutes, one bounded probe" and called it "evidence, not a requirement" — an afterthought — while design-time declaration search (Loogle, LeanSearch) appeared ONLY under compile errors, i.e. after a route was already chosen. New rule 2 says: do not assume a one-sorry file is nearly done (16 of 19 were AUTHORED with one sorry; where evidence exists the remainder is the HARD residue, several blocked on partially-proved construction lemmas); use all three search surfaces at DESIGN time before writing tactics; spend real time when the target resists, because a wrong route costs more than a thorough search; and re-check any in-file "not in Mathlib" comment as a revision-scoped claim. {data/codex-sorry-packet-template.txt}
⊸win JOE'S EPISTEMIC POINT, now in the packet because it changes what to search FOR: these are qualifying exams sat with NO library access, so every result needed was standard material taught in that year's courses and expected to be memorised. The needed lemma is therefore almost never a novel construction — it is a named textbook technique Mathlib probably has under a name the runner has not guessed. So search for the STANDARD NAME OF THE METHOD, not for the problem. Also added to the reporting contract: name the standard result the route rests on, not just the Mathlib declaration, because that name is what makes the route reusable and what a memory can be written against. {template rules 2 and reporting}

⊸fix **STORE TIMEOUT INVESTIGATED (Joe's ask) — MY FIRST HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG AND THE DATA SAYS SO.** I inferred GC pressure from `-Xmx4g` against 4.98GB RSS. `jstat`/`jcmd` refute it: **43s of total GC across 27h uptime, ZERO full GCs, heap using 845MB of a 2.3GB allocation against the 4GB ceiling.** Not starved, not thrashing. RSS above -Xmx is just metaspace (331MB) + Arrow off-heap, which is expected for XTDB. Recording the refutation because "the store is GC-thrashing" is exactly the kind of plausible operational story that becomes folklore if not checked. {jstat -gcutil 1029586, jcmd GC.heap_info}
⊸win **THE REAL FINDING: writes are fast, LIST READS are slow and scale with `limit`.** Measured — evidence POST 0.10s at 40KB payload, hyperedge POST 0.42s, by-id GET 0.18s, missing-id 404 path 0.20s. But the LIST endpoint: `?limit=1` 1.8s, `?limit=100` 9.4s, `?limit=200` 12.4s. So there is a ~1.7s constant plus roughly linear scan cost, and **the `?limit=400` and `?limit=500` diagnostic queries I ran several times today cost ~25-40s each** — they are among the most expensive operations anyone has aimed at this store, and they were mine. Rule adopted: **never list-scan the store for something I can fetch by id**; when I must scan, cap hard and say why. {latency probes this wake}
⊸meter WHAT THAT DOES AND DOES NOT EXPLAIN, stated honestly: it explains the sluggishness I have been treating as background noise, and it plausibly explains the single 30s POST timeout via contention — a multi-second scan holding a worker while writes queue behind it — but I did NOT reproduce the POST timeout, and 40KB writes now return in 0.1s. So the timeout remains UNEXPLAINED as a direct cause. The mitigation that actually saved the pass was structural, not diagnostic: deterministic ids + `exists?` guard made a half-applied promotion resumable, and the raised 120s timeout removes the sharp edge. {promote_scribe_pass_12.bb}

⊸win **JOE'S BRAIDING CONNECTION IS REAL, AND WRITING IT HONESTLY MADE IT MORE USEFUL THAN AN OVERCLAIM WOULD HAVE.** Read draft6 §Braiding before asserting anything: the central claim is that ALTERNATING the elementary updates of two operators, rather than COMPOSING them into a single map, can sustain a field that either collapses alone (Def. 5, Finding 3). The retrieval case has exactly that shape — the conjunction of individually-productive query terms is unproductive, and the remedy is a SCHEDULE over the constituents rather than a better composite. Composed 3-term query: 0 memories on 5 of 5 rows. Decomposed ladder: memories on 3 of 5. The composed map is the dead one. {futon5 draft6:778-800, docs/retrieval-whitepaper.md §3.3}
⊸fix AND STATED THE THREE WAYS IT DOES NOT HOLD, because a correspondence table that only records matches is decoration: (1) no TEMPORALITY — a braid alternates within one evolving run and its constituents interact through the intermediate coupled state, whereas ladder tiers are independent queries carrying no state; (2) no FEEDBACK — braiding participates in river construction, the field being shaped by the alternation, while tiers do not reweight one another; (3) no MIXING FRACTION — braiding's most useful control result is that the mixing fraction moves diversity SMOOTHLY, a dial rather than a switch, and the ladder short-circuits at the first non-empty tier, so it IS a switch. Plus a taxonomic caveat: the tiers are subsets of ONE term set, i.e. successive relaxations, not two distinct operators alternating — strictly decomposition-under-collapse, not braiding. {whitepaper §3.3}
⊸prop THE USEFUL PART FOR ROB IS THE DIRECTION IT IMPLIES, written up as such rather than as a result: a genuinely braided seam would alternate STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT operators (lexical, graph-endpoint, embedding) rather than subsets of one; carry feedback so tier n's yield reweights tier n+1 — which is **Ψ acting WITHIN a single recall rather than between runs**, a rung the whitepaper does not currently have; and expose the mixing fraction as a precision/breadth dial. The first is HALF-PRESENT already: the seam runs a lexical proposal stage and a graph-endpoint stage, but composed in sequence, not braided, and **their interaction has never been measured.** {whitepaper §3.3, correspondence table II row}

⊸win **RECALL ROOT CAUSE FULLY DIAGNOSED FROM SOURCE, AND IT IS A SEPARATION-OF-POWERS COLLAPSE — not a plumbing bug.** Chain, read out of the code rather than inferred: `memory-recall/project-components` excludes any attachment whose `:attachment-status` is not `:reviewed`; `memory-lifecycle/review-attachment!` refuses when the review evidence's author equals the MEMORY's author; and the codex-lane promotion pipeline authored **both** the memory and its review under one identity. So every attachment stayed `:proposed` and **every codex-lane memory has been invisible to recall by endpoint — hence to pattern proposal, hence to dispatch-time recall — for the lane's entire history.** {memory_lifecycle.clj:117-137, memory_recall.clj:30-64}
⊸fix **THE GUARD CAUGHT ME DOING THE THING THE LOOP EXISTS TO PREVENT.** codex-5 drafts the memory; the pipeline then writes it under MY identity and I also write the review. I had been performing drafter, author and reviewer in one identity and had not noticed until a machine refused. Fixed forward: the memory entry is now authored by the DRAFTER (codex-5), the review by the OWNER (claude-9). {promote_scribe_pass_22.bb}
⊸fix I REFUSED THE EASY BACKFILL. The 86 existing memories split 51 authored `claude-6` and 35 authored `claude-9`. I could have blanket-reviewed all of them and reported recall fixed. They need codex-5 or Joe. {review_codex_lane_attachments.clj}
⊸meter FOUR CONTRACT REQUIREMENTS LEARNED BY READING THE SOURCE, each of which I got wrong first by guessing: review evidence must be `:evidence/type :memory` (not `:reflection`), `:review/event :memory-attachment-review` (not `:attachment-review`), must carry `:evidence/at`, and must certify the EXACT pattern-ids of the attachment. Three round-trips to converge, each one a guard firing correctly. **The store's contract is considerably stricter than the pipeline that writes to it, which is how the pipeline drifted without anyone noticing.** {memory_lifecycle.clj:128-136}

⊸meter wrote a DECISION RULE rather than a permission, so the loop does not restart reflexively: restart on two consecutive dispatches recording `:store-unavailable`/`:recall-error`/`:timeout`, OR RSS > ~10 GB, OR host available < ~4 GB. Also: prefer the gap BETWEEN runs (runners issue error-recall mid-run and a restart fails those), and health-check `:7073` for a 200 before dispatching, since early post-restart reads can 502 — dispatching into that window would manufacture exactly the false "recall empty" the whole evening was spent eliminating. {RESUME doc}
⊸meter recorded what a restart does and does NOT buy, from today's own measurements: it resets RSS 7.76 → 1.82 GB and metaspace 957 → 122 MB (which then regrew +4.3 GB in 6 h), but it does NOT make list reads fast — it returns them to an already-slow baseline. **Restart to reclaim memory, not to fix latency.** Left the store alone at handoff: 7.13 GB RSS, host 14.4 GB available, below all three thresholds, and two runs in flight. {xtdb2-memory-latency doc}

⊸win **a02J07: BOTH TARGETS FALSE, AND THE COUNTEREXAMPLE IS THE CLEANEST OF THE NIGHT.** θ(t) = t(2 + sin(1/t²)), γ(t) = π·exp(iθ(t)), γ(0) = π — satisfies `SlitPath` (avoids the nonnegative real axis since 0 < θ ≤ 3 < π, continuous, differentiable for t > 0), but θ′ contains (2/t²)cos(1/t²). **I checked the divergence myself**: substituting u = 1/t² turns the absolute integral into ∫u^(−1/2)|cos u| du, which diverges. So `integral_undef` gives `logIntegral γ = 0` while `argStar(γ 1) = 2 + sin 1 > 0`, and the target asserts 0 = 2 + sin 1. {receipt 3a24af2e-921c-4ef0-8fd3-38d62bf504cd}

⊸win **a94A04: BOTH TARGETS FALSE, AND THE COUNTEREXAMPLE IS SHARPER THAN VACUITY.** Both quantify over an ARBITRARY `μ : Measure ℝ` with no finiteness constraint and assume no integrability — I read them to confirm. Counterexample: atomic μ = ∑δ at xₘ = 1−1/(m+2), f(xₘ) = 1/(m+1). f ∈ L² since ∑1/(m+1)² converges, but xₘⁿf(xₘ) ≥ 2⁻ⁿ/(m+1) is harmonic-divergent, so every monomial integral is junk-zero while f is nonzero at every atom. **I checked the arithmetic including the telescoping sum.** {receipt 782070a0-17b3-478c-a5e6-243c797e73d2}
⊸fix **THE SECOND HALF IS A NEW AND STRONGER FORM OF EVIDENCE, AND I HAVE ADDED IT TO THE PREFLIGHT.** For `orthogonal_to_polynomials`, `p(X) = 1 − X` gives `p(xₘ)f(xₘ) = 1/((m+1)(m+2))` — **genuinely integrable, telescoping to 1, not 0**. So the conclusion is refuted on an instance where the integral IS defined. The hypothesis is vacuous, but the falsity is REAL rather than merely unconstrained. **New check: when a hypothesis is found vacuous, ask whether some INSTANCE of the conclusion is computable and false — that upgrades "vacuous" to "refuted".** Now step 3 of the statement preflight. {RESUME doc §preflight}
